\doxysubsection*{\+: the name chosen to select it on the available\+\_\+tracers file}
\label{_amgrpb9a64d7bd016c76b440cf939d5a7ff26}%
struct tracer -\/ a specific tracer and its callbacks to interact with tracefs

@init\+: called when one switches to this tracer (echo name $>$ current\+\_\+tracer) @reset\+: called when one switches to another tracer @start\+: called when tracing is unpaused (echo 1 $>$ tracing\+\_\+on) @stop\+: called when tracing is paused (echo 0 $>$ tracing\+\_\+on) @update\+\_\+thresh\+: called when tracing\+\_\+thresh is updated @open\+: called when the trace file is opened @pipe\+\_\+open\+: called when the trace\+\_\+pipe file is opened @close\+: called when the trace file is released @pipe\+\_\+close\+: called when the trace\+\_\+pipe file is released @read\+: override the default read callback on trace\+\_\+pipe @splice\+\_\+read\+: override the default splice\+\_\+read callback on trace\+\_\+pipe @selftest\+: selftest to run on boot (see trace\+\_\+selftest.\+c) @print\+\_\+headers\+: override the first lines that describe your columns @print\+\_\+line\+: callback that prints a trace @set\+\_\+flag\+: signals one of your private flags changed (\mbox{\hyperlink{structtrace__options}{trace\+\_\+options}} file) @flags\+: your private flags \begin{DoxyCompactItemize}

\doxysubsection*{\+: The unique name that identifies the event command. This is}
\label{_amgrpfde6f827d63c8e9159ed602a36aa55a0}%
struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structevent__command}{event\+\_\+command}} -\/ callbacks and data members for event commands

Event commands are invoked by users by writing the command name into the \textquotesingle{}trigger\textquotesingle{} file associated with a trace event. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} parameters associated with a specific invocation of an event command are used to create an event trigger instance, which is added to the list of trigger instances associated with that trace event. When the event is hit, the set of triggers associated with that event is invoked.

\mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} data members in this structure provide per-\/event command data for various event commands.

All the data members below, except for @post\+\_\+trigger, must be set for each event command.

the name used when setting triggers via trigger files.

@trigger\+\_\+type\+: A unique id that identifies the event command \textquotesingle{}type\textquotesingle{}. This value has two purposes, the first to ensure that only one trigger of the same type can be set at a given time for a particular event e.\+g. it doesn\textquotesingle{}t make sense to have both a traceon and traceoff trigger attached to a single event at the same time, so traceon and traceoff have the same type though they have different names. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} @trigger\+\_\+type value is also used as a bit value for deferring the actual trigger action until after the current event is finished. Some commands need to do this if they themselves log to the trace buffer (see the @post\+\_\+trigger() member below). @trigger\+\_\+type values are defined by adding new values to the trigger\+\_\+type enum in \mbox{\hyperlink{linux_2trace__events_8h}{include/linux/trace\+\_\+events.\+h}}.

@flags\+: See the enum event\+\_\+command\+\_\+flags below.

All the methods below, except for @set\+\_\+filter() and @unreg\+\_\+all(), must be implemented.

@func\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} callback function responsible for parsing and registering the trigger written to the \textquotesingle{}trigger\textquotesingle{} file by the user. It allocates the trigger instance and registers it with the appropriate trace event. It makes use of the other \mbox{\hyperlink{structevent__command}{event\+\_\+command}} callback functions to orchestrate this, and is usually implemented by the generic utility function @event\+\_\+trigger\+\_\+callback() (see trace\+\_\+event\+\_\+triggers.\+c).

@reg\+: Adds the trigger to the list of triggers associated with the event, and enables the event trigger itself, after initializing it (via the \mbox{\hyperlink{structevent__trigger__ops}{event\+\_\+trigger\+\_\+ops}} @init() function). This is also where commands can use the @trigger\+\_\+type value to make the decision as to whether or not multiple instances of the trigger should be allowed. This is usually implemented by the generic utility function @register\+\_\+trigger() (see trace\+\_\+event\+\_\+triggers.\+c).

@unreg\+: Removes the trigger from the list of triggers associated with the event, and disables the event trigger itself, after initializing it (via the \mbox{\hyperlink{structevent__trigger__ops}{event\+\_\+trigger\+\_\+ops}} @free() function). This is usually implemented by the generic utility function @unregister\+\_\+trigger() (see trace\+\_\+event\+\_\+triggers.\+c).

@unreg\+\_\+all\+: An optional function called to remove all the triggers from the list of triggers associated with the event. Called when a trigger file is opened in truncate mode.

@set\+\_\+filter\+: An optional function called to parse and set a filter for the trigger. If no @set\+\_\+filter() method is set for the event command, filters set by the user for the command will be ignored. This is usually implemented by the generic utility function @set\+\_\+trigger\+\_\+filter() (see trace\+\_\+event\+\_\+triggers.\+c).

@get\+\_\+trigger\+\_\+ops\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} callback function invoked to retrieve the \mbox{\hyperlink{structevent__trigger__ops}{event\+\_\+trigger\+\_\+ops}} implementation associated with the command. \begin{DoxyCompactItemize}

\index{trace.h@{trace.h}!event\_command\_flags@{event\_command\_flags}}
\index{event\_command\_flags@{event\_command\_flags}!trace.h@{trace.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{event\_command\_flags}{event\_command\_flags}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{interfaceenum}{enum}} \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2trace_2trace_8h_a88cb1d00015cec8a1f259d1910cc5603}{event\+\_\+command\+\_\+flags}}}

enum event\+\_\+command\+\_\+flags -\/ flags for struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structevent__command}{event\+\_\+command}}

@\+P\+O\+S\+T\+\_\+\+T\+R\+I\+G\+G\+ER\+: A flag that says whether or not this command needs to have its action delayed until after the current event has been closed. Some triggers need to avoid being invoked while an event is currently in the process of being logged, since the trigger may itself log data into the trace buffer. Thus we make sure the current event is committed before invoking those triggers. To do that, the trigger invocation is split in two -\/ the first part checks the filter using the current trace record; if a command has the @post\+\_\+trigger flag set, it sets a bit for itself in the return value, otherwise it directly invokes the trigger. Once all commands have been either invoked or set their return flag, the current record is either committed or discarded. At that point, if any commands have deferred their triggers, those commands are finally invoked following the close of the current event. In other words, if the \mbox{\hyperlink{structevent__trigger__ops}{event\+\_\+trigger\+\_\+ops}} @func() probe implementation itself logs to the trace buffer, this flag should be set, otherwise it can be left unspecified.

@\+N\+E\+E\+D\+S\+\_\+\+R\+EC\+: A flag that says whether or not this command needs access to the trace record in order to perform its function, regardless of whether or not it has a filter associated with it (filters make a trigger require access to the trace record but are not always present). \begin{DoxyEnumFields}{Enumerator}
\raisebox{\heightof{T}}[0pt][0pt]{\index{EVENT\_CMD\_FL\_POST\_TRIGGER@{EVENT\_CMD\_FL\_POST\_TRIGGER}!trace.h@{trace.h}}\index{trace.h@{trace.h}!EVENT\_CMD\_FL\_POST\_TRIGGER@{EVENT\_CMD\_FL\_POST\_TRIGGER}}}\mbox{\Hypertarget{kernel_2trace_2trace_8h_a88cb1d00015cec8a1f259d1910cc5603a17d3f9c3d675dd4cb22128bc9101602c}\label{kernel_2trace_2trace_8h_a88cb1d00015cec8a1f259d1910cc5603a17d3f9c3d675dd4cb22128bc9101602c}}

clear\+\_\+event\+\_\+triggers -\/ Clear all triggers associated with a trace array @tr\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} trace array to clear

For each trigger, the triggering event has its tm\+\_\+ref decremented via \mbox{\hyperlink{trace__events__trigger_8c_ab613b91b2cf35e69c8869e9d6f44715d}{trace\+\_\+event\+\_\+trigger\+\_\+enable\+\_\+disable()}}, and any associated event (in the case of enable/disable\+\_\+event triggers) will have its sm\+\_\+ref decremented via \mbox{\hyperlink{decompress_2mm_8h_aa7157aa9480bb73f090dca36776419bf}{free()}}-\/$>$\mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2trace_2trace_8h_aec113fbb6d3c703f2b4ce3dd89930d1a}{trace\+\_\+event\+\_\+enable\+\_\+disable()}}. That combination effectively reverses the soft-\/mode/trigger state added by trigger registration.

event\+\_\+trace\+\_\+add\+\_\+tracer -\/ add a instance of a \mbox{\hyperlink{structtrace__array}{trace\+\_\+array}} to events @parent\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} parent dentry to place the files/directories for events in @tr\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} trace array associated with these events

When a new instance is created, it needs to set up its events directory, as well as other files associated with events. It also creates the event hierarchy in the @parent/events directory.

Returns 0 on success.

Must be called with event\+\_\+mutex held. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

filter\+\_\+parse\+\_\+regex -\/ parse a basic regex @buff\+: the raw regex @len\+: length of the regex @search\+: will point to the beginning of the string to compare @not\+: tell whether the match will have to be inverted

This passes in a buffer containing a regex and this function will set search to point to the search part of the buffer and return the type of search it is (see enum above). This does modify buff.

Returns enum type. search returns the pointer to use for comparison. not returns 1 if buff started with a \textquotesingle{}!\textquotesingle{} 0 otherwise. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

pause\+\_\+named\+\_\+trigger -\/ Pause all named triggers with the same name @data\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} trigger data of a named trigger to pause

Pauses a named trigger along with all other triggers having the same name. Because named triggers share a common set of data, pausing only one is meaningless, so pausing one named trigger needs to pause all triggers with the same name. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

set\+\_\+named\+\_\+trigger\+\_\+data -\/ Associate common named trigger data @data\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} trigger data to associate @named\+\_\+data\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} common named trigger to be associated

Named triggers are sets of triggers that share a common set of trigger data. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} first named trigger registered with a given name owns the common trigger data that the others subsequently registered with the same name will reference. This function associates the common trigger data from the first trigger with the given trigger. Here is the caller graph for this function\+:

trace\+\_\+buffered\+\_\+event\+\_\+disable -\/ disable buffering events

When a filter is removed, it is faster to not use the buffered events, and to commit directly into the ring buffer. Free up the temp buffers when there are no more users. This requires special synchronization with current events. Here is the call graph for this function\+:
% FIG 119
Here is the caller graph for this function\+:
% FIG 120
\mbox{\Hypertarget{kernel_2trace_2trace_8h_a27ef435a51e44a602740f4118e6db40a}\label{kernel_2trace_2trace_8h_a27ef435a51e44a602740f4118e6db40a}} 
\index{trace.h@{trace.h}!trace\_buffered\_event\_enable@{trace\_buffered\_event\_enable}}
\index{trace\_buffered\_event\_enable@{trace\_buffered\_event\_enable}!trace.h@{trace.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{trace\_buffered\_event\_enable()}{trace\_buffered\_event\_enable()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily void trace\+\_\+buffered\+\_\+event\+\_\+enable (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{ }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

trace\+\_\+buffered\+\_\+event\+\_\+enable -\/ enable buffering events

When events are being filtered, it is quicker to use a temporary buffer to write the event data into if there\textquotesingle{}s a likely chance that it will not be committed. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} discard of the ring buffer is not as fast as committing, and is much slower than copying a commit.

When an event is to be filtered, allocate per cpu buffers to write the event data into, and if the event is filtered and discarded it is simply dropped, otherwise, the entire data is to be committed in one shot. Here is the call graph for this function\+:
% FIG 121

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{trace\_check\_vprintf()}{trace\_check\_vprintf()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily void trace\+\_\+check\+\_\+vprintf (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structtrace__iterator}{trace\+\_\+iterator}} $\ast$}]{iter,  }\item[{const char $\ast$}]{fmt,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{stdarg_8h_a2ed76bdaf83448883f275ac530e804e9}{va\+\_\+list}}}]{ap }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

trace\+\_\+check\+\_\+vprintf -\/ Check dereferenced strings while writing to the seq buffer @iter\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} iterator that holds the seq buffer and the event being printed @fmt\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} format used to print the event @ap\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} va\+\_\+list holding the data to print from @fmt.

This writes the data into the @iter-\/$>$seq buffer using the data from @fmt and @ap. If the format has a s, then the source of the string is examined to make sure it is safe to print, otherwise it will warn and print \char`\"{}\mbox{[}\+U\+N\+S\+A\+F\+E M\+E\+M\+O\+R\+Y\mbox{]}\char`\"{} in place of the dereferenced string pointer. {\footnotesize\ttfamily void trace\+\_\+filter\+\_\+add\+\_\+remove\+\_\+task (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structtrace__pid__list}{trace\+\_\+pid\+\_\+list}} $\ast$}]{pid\+\_\+list,  }\item[{struct task\+\_\+struct $\ast$}]{self,  }\item[{struct task\+\_\+struct $\ast$}]{task }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

trace\+\_\+filter\+\_\+add\+\_\+remove\+\_\+task -\/ Add or remove a task from a pid\+\_\+list @pid\+\_\+list\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} list to modify @self\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} current task for fork or N\+U\+LL for exit @task\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} task to add or remove

If adding a task, if @self is defined, the task is only added if @self is also included in @pid\+\_\+list. This happens on fork and tasks should only be added when the parent is listed. If @self is N\+U\+LL, then the @task pid will be removed from the list, which would happen on exit of a task. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{dcache_8h_ad65b480f8c8270356b45a9890f6499ae}{int}} tracing\+\_\+is\+\_\+enabled (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{ }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

tracing\+\_\+is\+\_\+enabled -\/ Show if global\+\_\+trace has been enabled

Shows if the global trace has been enabled or not. It uses the mirror flag \char`\"{}buffer\+\_\+disabled\char`\"{} to be used in fast paths such as for the irqsoff tracer. But it may be inaccurate due to races. If you need to know the accurate state, use \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2trace_2trace_8c_ab66b1dd4be2fc3fa5b3678f173599349}{tracing\+\_\+is\+\_\+on()}} which is a little slower, but accurate. \index{tracing\_log\_err@{tracing\_log\_err}!trace.h@{trace.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{tracing\_log\_err()}{tracing\_log\_err()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily void \mbox{\hyperlink{structtracing__log__err}{tracing\+\_\+log\+\_\+err}} (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structtrace__array}{trace\+\_\+array}} $\ast$}]{tr,  }\item[{const char $\ast$}]{loc,  }\item[{const char $\ast$}]{cmd,  }\item[{const char $\ast$$\ast$}]{errs,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{build_8c_aed742c436da53c1080638ce6ef7d13de}{u8}}}]{type,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{build_8c_aed742c436da53c1080638ce6ef7d13de}{u8}}}]{pos }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

\mbox{\hyperlink{structtracing__log__err}{tracing\+\_\+log\+\_\+err}} -\/ write an error to the tracing error log @tr\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} associated trace array for the error (N\+U\+LL for top level array) @loc\+: A string describing where the error occurred @cmd\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} tracing command that caused the error @errs\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} array of loc-\/specific static error strings @type\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} index into errs\mbox{[}\mbox{]}, which produces the specific static err string @pos\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} position the caret should be placed in the cmd

Writes an error into tracing/error\+\_\+log of the form\+:

$<$loc$>$\+: error\+: $<$text$>$ Command\+: $<$cmd$>$ $^\wedge$

tracing/error\+\_\+log is a small log file containing the last T\+R\+A\+C\+I\+N\+G\+\_\+\+L\+O\+G\+\_\+\+E\+R\+R\+S\+\_\+\+M\+AX errors (8). Memory for errors isn\textquotesingle{}t allocated unless there has been a tracing error, and the error log can be cleared and have its memory freed by writing the empty string in truncation mode to it i.\+e. echo $>$ tracing/error\+\_\+log.

N\+O\+TE\+: the @errs array along with the @type param are used to produce a static error string -\/ this string is not copied and saved when the error is logged -\/ only a pointer to it is saved. See existing callers for examples of how static strings are typically defined for use with \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2trace_2trace_8c_a00454c6a4e1af22dc8f3ad466198ea3b}{tracing\+\_\+log\+\_\+err()}}. tracing\+\_\+update\+\_\+buffers -\/ used by tracing facility to expand ring buffers

To save on memory when the tracing is never used on a system with it configured in. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} ring buffers are set to a minimum size. But once a user starts to use the tracing facility, then they need to grow to their default size.

This function is to be called when a tracer is about to be used. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

unpause\+\_\+named\+\_\+trigger -\/ Un-\/pause all named triggers with the same name @data\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} trigger data of a named trigger to unpause

Un-\/pauses a named trigger along with all other triggers having the same name. Because named triggers share a common set of data, unpausing only one is meaningless, so unpausing one named trigger needs to unpause all triggers with the same name. Here is the call graph for this function\+:
